Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life around the time this foggy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but this whole shift kind of started after my uncle died, about two weeks ago. I’ve been trying to keep things moving like normal, but that’s around when I noticed the fogginess more.\n\nDoctor: Has your sadness mostly been about missing your uncle?\n\nPatient: I think missing him is part of it, yeah… but it doesn’t feel only like that, if I’m being honest. I mean, I worry about normal stuff—work, making sure I’m doing right by people—but I wouldn’t say I’ve been worrying a lot. At least not more than usual. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How have you been sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: It’s been kind of uneven, I guess. Not terrible every single night, but… some nights I’m just lying there thinking about family stuff, and other times I wake up before my alarm and then I can’t really get settled again. I’m still functioning, though. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but that’s kind of part of why I’m here. My head’s been feeling foggy, and I’ve been making small mistakes in my client notes or walking into a room and forgetting what I was about to do. It’s not huge, but it’s not really like me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Did something stressful happen in your life around the time this foggy feeling started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but this whole shift kind of started after my uncle died, about two weeks ago. I’ve been trying to keep things moving like normal, but that’s around when I noticed the fogginess more.\n\nDoctor: Has your sadness mostly been about missing your uncle?\n\nPatient: I think missing him is part of it, yeah… but it doesn’t feel only like that, if I’m being honest. Lately I’ll wake up with this kind of heavy, empty feeling in my chest. I can still get through my shift and talk to people, so I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but inside I just feel sad and kind of flat. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Have you been getting less enjoyment than usual from things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I still do things if they’re there, like I’ll put on a show or try to relax, but I haven’t really been paying attention to whether I’m enjoying them less. It might be a little, but I don’t want to say that for sure if I’m not sure. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or like you’re a worthless person lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I think I’m worthless exactly. But I do keep going over what I should’ve done for my tío before he died—like calling him more, or visiting sooner. And then I start feeling like I failed him, like I wasn’t there the way family should be. I don’t know if that counts as guilt, but it’s been on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t have any plan to hurt myself or anything like that. I want to be clear about that. But, um… there have been some mornings where I wake up and think, like, it wouldn’t be the worst thing if I just didn’t wake up. And then I feel scared that I even thought that. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.
